To date, I have never heard of you, the Greens, that payments from Slovenia to the Group of People in Austria would be a non-peaceful contribution to strengthening the Group of People in Austria. Otherwise you will see this clearly when vice versa Austria supports its ethnic groups. After all, we know from the report by Professor Karners that about 1,860 persons living in German mother tongues as an old-Austrian minority are still living in the Gottschee and Lower Austria in the Laibach and Cilli area and are not enjoying full minority rights such as the Italians, the Croatians and Hungary in Slovenia, but are simply subsumed in general citizenship rights. This means that no schooling in their language, no representation in the Slovenian Parliament, no corresponding funding, no restoration of their seized property. I already believe that it is understandable that Austria has made a contribution to these remains of the old-Austrian people's group, which in the 1910 census still includes 224,000 people alone in the Lower Austria market and more than 34,000 people in the Gottschee, which is the payment of a long-term debt of thanks to the strategic survival structure of the old Austrians in Slovenia. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! We are in line with the Council of Europe's minority rights, because I should like to point out to you, ladies and gentlemen of the Greens, that, of course, the Council of Europe in the Declaration of Minority has made the promotion of not only existing, but also the restoration of former autochthonic minorities its promotion objectives. So if, for the first time, the Republic of Austria is ready to act in this Second Republic, in accordance with European law and with the corresponding resolutions and declarations of the Council of Europe, and if you, the Green Group, are criticised, I believe, you are at least questionable in your position of representing human rights for all people in Europe. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] [[Abg. Dr. Martin Graf: For me too!]] And something else, ladies and gentlemen of the Green Group: If you were in Slovenia as we were more often, you would also know – here I refer to Mr Lunacek – that the position of Mr. Chancellor, who meant that the old regime had now returned to the government with the current Slovenian government, is well justified. Since the elections in Slovenia and in the run-up to the elections, those groups that have received the majority in Slovenia during the vote have tried to exercise massive economic pressure, massive physical pressure and massive moral pressure on the existing old-Austrian minorities, especially in the Gottschee, in the run-up to the census that will take place in Slovenia in 2001, perhaps precisely because of the fact that the old government in Slovenia once meant that, on the basis of the closed settlement territory of the old-Austrian people in the Gottschee, it is possible to promote the Gottschee according to the minority rights of Article 81 of the Slovenian Constitution as a strengthening of minority rights. The new government has begun to exert massive pressure on the Gottscheer and its chairman Grill in the run-up to the election, and I believe that all Austrian parliamentarians should feel as a protective force for the old Austrians and condemn this approach by the Slovenian Government, but also by over-zealous nationalist representatives, who are now in the majority in Slovenia. The first is the double taxation agreement with the Republic of Finland. The old agreement dates back to 1963. The present recasting of the agreement not only meets the current and present standard of tax law, but it is also urgently necessary because large mutual investment exists. There is also the agreement with Azerbaijan. There has so far been no such agreement with this country. Now it may be said that Azerbaijan may not be that important, but I think it is particularly important to create timely tax security and legal certainty with markets and countries that will only be of importance to us in the future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] The agreement with Russia. The previous double taxation agreement has still been concluded with the Soviet Union. The agreement with Switzerland is particularly important. In this we finally reduced the withholding tax to 5 percent, for profit distributions on Group companies. In this respect, we really needed to rethink, because there have already been corresponding reductions in other important EU countries, or there is no source tax on corporate profits in many EU countries at all. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to conclude and ask all of you and ask you to give your consent to these very important bilateral agreements, namely the double taxation agreements. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-10-19-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00041_d7e19286	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The Austrian Federal Government is also committed to debt reduction this time in the poorest developing countries of our world. We take over – despite budget consolidation! – the responsibility to help these countries, and participate in the International Debug Initiative HIPC. The law to be passed is to relieve those countries which are unable to deal with their debts by their own means, so that they can again operate independently and, above all, invest in health and training. The control of this is carried out by the International Monetary Fund and the World Bank in order to ensure that the funds are used well. Social balance is also controlled in developing countries. This is how new partners for the economy grow. There is an acute need for action, as the debt stock was already 71 billion US dollars in 1999, and this must be reduced. The cost of this initiative is about $28.2 billion after the last calculations. The Austrian contribution is about 170 million Schilling. The International Monetary Fund has sold parts of its gold reserves to finance its share – which is about half of the costs. We believe that we have made a valuable contribution, because only those who help quickly help twice as you know. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-10-19-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00041_d7e19386	The next speaker is Mrs Jäger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-10-19-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00041_d7e19407	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! According to the United Nations Development Assistance Organisation, the developing countries have about $50 billion each year in funding for development cooperation. In the same period, approximately $500 billion of return flows from developing countries to industrialized countries are recorded. This happens through unfavourable trade relations, but above all through the capital outflow that comes about in the context of indebtedness. Ladies and gentlemen! Willy Brandt once spoke of debt service as the "blood transfusion from the sick to the healthy". I therefore welcome this government template because it is a matter of finally taking a step in the field of industrialized countries to remove these debts. In principle, I welcome the HIPC initiative – an initiative by the World Bank and the International Monetary Fund – in which poverty reduction is addressed by removing debts. However, I must say that it is too slow that it is too little effective. Twenty countries were promised to be included in this debt initiative by the end of this year. At present, I believe, we are standing in nine countries. Whether the World Bank and the International Monetary Fund will still be able to do so, we will see. The solution to the debt problem is an integral part of development aid. Sustainable development without a lasting solution to over-indebtedness is impossible in most developing countries. I would like to thank very much, in this context, an organisation which is also active in Austria. It is this - my colleague Lunacek has already mentioned it - the organisation "Jubilee 2000 UK". This organization operates worldwide. It has collected 21 million signatures worldwide in this year and then presented them in New York in September at the UN Millennium Summit. This represents the strongest petition of history ever performed. I think this is a great achievement, which shows that many people are aware that the debt of the poorest countries is really the great scandal, because these debts have long been paid out in the course of interest subsidies. Therefore, there is a need for initiatives to really help the poorest people in the world. I would now like to mention a number of these. Germany, for example, received debt in 1953, and what is now being offered to the countries of the Third World is at least three times as high as the debt ratio that was offered to Germany at the time, with 150 percent of the ratio of debt to exports. Finally, I would like to say: If we really want to solve the problems of the countries of the south in the future, debt is an important prerequisite for this.

Let me briefly consider what this is really all about. With this request we want to achieve that in Austria the broker commissions are lowered when renting an apartment or when buying a home. The Austrian housing seekers have to pay the highest broker commissions throughout the EU on the European average. I would like to tell you the level of broker commissions in other EU Member States so that you can know about the sizes. If an Austrian family goes to a real estate agent to ensure that it has a roof above the head, on average three gross monthly rents must be paid, which is about 20 800 S. A German family has an average of 12 800 S. In Finland it is only 8 000 S at all, in France a family pays for the placement of a rental apartment 4 300 S and in Sweden 1 800 S. I call these figures because I think it is necessary for this federal government to look at the examples of other Member States in this regard, because I believe that it is possible to take a good example in other countries where housing seekers have to pay much less than in Austria, unfortunately. We have the highest broker commissions, and there is a need for action, and that is because broker commissions are, of course, part of the housing costs and, above all, the FPÖ has undertaken to reduce these costs! We have also experienced in this regard that the opposite is the case now, and you can now take the proof of truth on which side you are actually standing, whether you are concerned about the problems of the tenants or whether you are primarily representing the clients of real estate agents – some of you are real estate agents – whether you are so to speak the shirt is closer than the skirt. There are many such close relations with the FPÖ, and therefore there is in reality no willingness to change anything in the area of housing cost reduction. It should also be noted that for the first time in the Second Republic in social housing, namely in the state-owned housing cooperative, the Supervisory Board Chairman is a real estate agent, one who wants to do business. I just hope he doesn't want to do business with social housing and with tenants! Wolfgang Pirklhuberand comrades concerning a federal law amending the Agricultural Law (191/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2000-10-19-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00041_d7e20769	We arrive at the 15th. Agenda item. The debate is open. I call Mr Pirklhuber. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-10-19-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00041_d7e20796	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! The present Green Initiative proposal deals with the current challenges of the current agricultural policy strategies for the future. It is not only in the interests of farmers that it is necessary to integrate the ecologicalisation clearly and precisely into the agricultural law, but also consumers have a right to make clear that the objectives of agricultural policy are clearly stated that greening not only remains lip recognition, but it is clear, precise statements in this regard. Finally, it is a matter of developing a model for agriculture. Organic farming as a production method, which has not only been repeatedly propagated by us and has been represented here in Parliament for years, is not a production method among others. No, no, no, This is an agricultural method which is also uniformly regulated in EU Regulation 2092/91. This makes organic farming the only agricultural method in the world, which is really based on clearly defined rules, which is clearly regulated. Bio-agriculture is an economic mode that is efficient in terms of important environmental objectives, such as groundwater protection, drinking water protection, biodiversity and soil protection, and of course, ladies and gentlemen, also with regard to animal welfare and animal welfare. All of this is included in the biological economy. Ladies and gentlemen! It is therefore a matter of integrating this economic approach into the Austrian Agricultural Act. We already have such a law in Austria, namely the Vienna Agricultural Act, which was raised from baptism only in November last year. At the initiative of the Greens, organic farming is already an essential part of this Vienna agricultural law, a clear objective of agricultural and agricultural policy measures. Ladies and gentlemen! In addition, the question of genetic engineering must also be clarified. Why? – Bioagriculture is free of genetic engineering by definition, on the basis of the EU regulation. Minister Molterer has said again and again and again, saying that we are the pioneers, despite the problems of exit, and that we have in organic farming, and if we want to secure this and take this pioneering seriously, we must also seriously consider how we can secure the freedom of genetic engineering in Austria. Seed production for organic farming is only sustainable if we are free of genetic engineering in our national production area – at least try to secure it. This is EU-wide, that is possible. In accordance with the EU Release Directive, there is the possibility of demarcating geographical areas without genetic engineering. This is precisely what is trying to link this initiative proposal: to integrate the interests of agriculture and the interests of consumers with a clear target formulation for the next years and decades for the Austrian agricultural policy into the Agricultural Law. Accept this challenge, ladies and gentlemen from the agricultural side, accept this challenge! Freedom of genetic engineering is also a great opportunity for agriculture itself. Now we can still realize that; in ten years it will be difficult. [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign.]] [[Abg. Haigermoser: Sit down!]] I therefore urge you to support the Committee's discussions with constructive and positive contributions. - Thank you.
